Intuitionistic monotone modal logic via translation
Jim de Groot

Abstract
We introduce a monotone modal analogue of the intuitionistic (normal) modal logic 𝖨𝖪 \mathsf{IK} using a translation into a suitable (intuitionistic) first-order logic. We axiomatise the logic and give a semantics by means of intuitionistic neighbourhood models, which contain neighbourhoods whose value can change when moving along the intuitionistic accessibility relation. We compare the resulting logic with other intuitionistic monotone modal logics and show how it can be embedded into a multimodal version of 𝖨𝖪 \mathsf{IK} .



1  Introduction

Ever since Fitch first introduced a modal extension of intuitionistic
logic
[
22
]
,
many different systems of intuitionistic modal logic have been put forward
and investigated
[
6
,
8
,
20
,
21
,
5
,
19
,
38
,
40
,
46
,
47
,
48
,
16
]
.
The various approaches often differ on the relation between the box and diamond modality.
On one side of this spectrum sit for example logics studied by
Sotirov
[
41
, Section 4]
and Wolter and Zakharyaschev
[
48
, Section 2]
,
in which
and
are completely unrelated.
At the other extremity, we have systems
studied by Bull
[
7
]
and
Božić and Došen
[
5
, Section 11]
,
in which the diamond operator can be viewed as an abbreviation for
\neg\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\neg
.

There are two prominent ways to establish a more subtle interaction between
the modalities: via a proof system and via a (standard) translation.
These give rise to
constructive
and
intuitionistic
counterparts of
classical modal logics.
Constructive versions of the classical normal modal logic

\mathsf{K}
were introduced by Bellin, De Paiva and
Ritter
[
2
]
and by Wijesekera
[
44
]
,
and are obtained by restricting the sequent calculus for
\mathsf{K}

to single conclusions, or to zero or one conclusions.
Besides their proof-theoretic perspectives, both logics are also
motivated by applications in AI:
\mathsf{CK}
captures the notion of contex
in knowledge representation and reasoning
[
18
,
37
]

and
\mathsf{WK}
can be used to represent states with partial knowledge
[
45
]
.

Intuitionistic
\mathsf{K}
(
\mathsf{IK}
) was introduced by Fischer Servi
[
20
,
21
]
,
and further studied by Plotkin and Sterling
[
38
]
, Ewald
[
19
]

and Simpson
[
40
]
. It can be viewed as the set of formulas whose
standard translation is derivable in
intuitionistic
first-order
logic
[
40
]
, or equivalently, whose translation into a suitable
classical bimodal logic is derivable
[
20
]
.
The relation between
\mathsf{CK}
,
\mathsf{IK}
and various logics in between
is investigated in
[
15
,
17
]
.

Besides intuitionistic analogues of
\mathsf{K}
,
extensions of intuitionistic logic with monotone modal operators have
been studied as well. Monotone modal operators are modalities
\triangledown
for
which
\varphi\to\psi
implies
\triangledown\varphi\to\triangledown\psi
, or equivalently,
that satisfy
\triangledown(\varphi\wedge\psi)\to\triangledown\varphi
.
The study of these in an intuitionistic context dates back to
Bull
[
6
, Page 5]
, who studied a monotone modality that
satisfies the S4 axioms.
Discarding the S4 axioms yields Goldblatt’s intuitionistic logic
with a geometric modality
[
25
,
26
]
, denoted here by
\mathsf{iM}
,
which was further researched in e.g.
[
31
,
42
,
30
]
.
More recently, extensions of intuitionistic logic with two monotone modal operators were
investigated by Dalmonte, Grellois and Olivetti
[
13
,
14
]
.
Some of these can be viewed as
constructive
monotone modal logics,
in the sense that they can be obtained by restricting a sequent calculus
for classical monotone modal logic
[
12
]
.
Surprisingly, it appears that intuitionistic monotone modal logics arising
from a translation into a suitable intuitionistic first-order logic have
not yet been studied. This is the gap we are aiming to fill in this paper.

The first step towards this goal is to decide on a suitable first-order logic.
Since monotone modal logic is usually interpreted in neighbourhood
models and we need our first-order structures to mimic these,
we use a two-sorted first-order logic
\mathsf{FOM}
, with the
first sort representing worlds and the second representing neighbourhoods.
These are related using predicates
\mathsf{N}
and
\mathsf{E}
,
where
w\mathsf{N}a
and
a\mathsf{E}w
are intuitively read as “
a
is a neighbourhood of
w
”
and “
w
is an element of
a
,” respectively.
This way of viewing neighbourhood structures stems from
[
23
]
,
and has since been used regularly
[
32
,
9
,
34
,
29
]
.
It can be shown that classical monotone modal logic is the set of formulas
whose standard translation is derivable in
\mathsf{FOM}
.
We then obtain an intuitionistic monotone modal logic as the set of formulas
whose standard translation is derivable in
\mathsf{IFOM}
, the intuitionistic
first-order logic with the same signature as
\mathsf{FOM}
.
In fact, to give us more flexibility, we define the logic
\mathsf{IM}
as a collection
of consecutions, i.e. expressions of the form
\Gamma\vdash\varphi
where
\Gamma
is a set
of formulas and
\varphi
is a single formula.

The next objective is to axiomatise
\mathsf{IM}
.
It turns out that
\mathsf{IM}
is stronger than its constructive counterpart,
and that it can be obtained by extending the logic
\mathsf{WM}
from
[
12
]

with the interaction axiom

(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\top\to\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi)\to\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi
.
This mirrors the fact that
\mathsf{IK}
can be obtained by extending
\mathsf{WK}
.
In order to prove completeness of the axiomatisation, we use a semantic detour.
We know that
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures (trivially) provide a sound and complete semantics
for
\mathsf{IM}
. Guided by these, we define
intuitionistic neighbourhood models

and we show that these validate precisely the same sequents as the
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures.
Completeness then follows from a routine canonical model construction
where we build an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

An intuitionistic neighbourhood model consist of a (partially ordered)
intuitionistic Kripke frame, a valuation, and a set of

intuitionistic neighbourhoods
: these are neighbourhoods that can
change when moving along the intuitionistic accessibility relation.
This reflects the universal quantification over the “neighbourhood sort”
of
\mathsf{IFOM}
, which relies on the shape of the neighbourhood in all accessible worlds.
When viewing neighbourhoods as representing evidence, intuitionistic
neighbourhoods embody the idea that a piece evidence may change when
moving among possible worlds.
Building the bridge between
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures and intuitionistic
neighbourhood models is one of the main technical challenges of the paper.

In an effort to position the logic
\mathsf{IM}
into the logical landscape,
we compare it to various other intuitionistic modal logics.
First, we consider the embedding of
\mathsf{iM}
into the diamond-free fragments and box-free
fragments of
\mathsf{WM}
and
\mathsf{IM}
. This gives rise to various (non-)conservativity
results, again mirroring the relation between constructive and
intuitionistic
\mathsf{K}

[
15
,
17
]
.
Second, we show that
\mathsf{IM}
can be embedded into a multimodal version of
\mathsf{IK}
,
in the sense that a sequent is derivable in
\mathsf{IM}
if and only if
its multimodal translation is derivable.
This can be viewed as an intuitionistic analogue of the embedding of
classical monotone modal logic into bimodal
\mathsf{K}
,
which is investigated in
[
32
, Section 5.2]
and in more
generality in
[
24
,
35
]
.

Structure of the paper

In Section
2
we define the logic
\mathsf{IM}

using the translation into the intuitionistic first-order logic
\mathsf{IFOM}
.
In Section
3
we investigate various semantics for
\mathsf{IM}
,
and use a canonical model construction to prove completeness.
Then, in Section
4
, we investigate the relation between

\mathsf{iM}
and fragments of
\mathsf{WM}
and
\mathsf{IM}
,
and we show how to embed
\mathsf{IM}
into multimodal
\mathsf{IK}
.
We conclude in Section
5
.

2  From classical to intuitionistic monotone modal logic

As outlined in the introduction, in order to obtain an intuitionistic counterpart
of monotone modal logic, we start by taking a first-order perspective on classical
monotone modal logic and then change the first-order logic to an
intuitionistic one.

Throughout this paper, we let

of proposition letters.
Note that this can be the language underlying both classical an intuitionistic
modal logics.

2.1  Classical monotone modal logic

Monotone modal logic
\mathsf{M}
is the extension of classical propositional
logic with two dual monotone modalities
and

[
11
,
32
,
33
]
.
That is, it arises from extending an axiomatisation of classical propositional logic
with the duality axiom
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\leftrightarrow\neg\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}

and the monotonicity rules

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt}}).

The logic is usually interpreted in neighbourhood semantics.
There are various ways to do so. Most importantly, the monotonicity of the
modalities can either be built into the frames
[
11
,
32
]
,
or into their interpretation
[
36
,
1
]
.
We use the latter, as this aligns best with our first-order intentions.

2.1 Definition
.

A
neighbourhood frame
is a pair
(W,N)
consisting of a nonempty
set
W
of worlds and a
neighbourhood function

\gamma:W\to\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}(W)
,
where
\mathcal{P}(W)
denotes the powerset of
W
.
A
neighbourhood model
is a tuple
\mathcal{M}=(W,\gamma,V)
consisting of a
neighbourhood frame
(W,\gamma)
and a valuation
V:\Prop\to\mathcal{P}(W)

of the proposition letters.
The interpretation of
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
-formulas at a world
w
in a neighbourhood
model
\mathcal{M}=(W,N,V)
is given recursively by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash p

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad w\in V(p)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\bot

never

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\wedge\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\text{ and }\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\psi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\vee\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\text{ or }\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\psi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M},w\not\Vdash\varphi\text{ or }\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\psi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{there exists }a\in\gamma(w)\text{ such that for all }v\in a\text{ we have }\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }a\in\gamma(w)\text{ there exists }v\in a\text{ such that }\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi

We use a two-sorted first-order correspondence language, with one sort representing
the worlds and the other the neighbourhoods
[
23
,
34
,
29
]
.

2.2 Definition
.

Let
\mathsf{FOM}
be the two-sorted first-order logic with sorts
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{n}
,
a binary predicate
\mathsf{N}
between
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{n}
,
a binary predicate
\mathsf{E}
between
\mathsf{n}
and
\mathsf{s}
,
and a unary predicate
\mathsf{P}_{i}
of type
\mathsf{s}
for each
p_{i}\in\Prop
.

Then a
\mathsf{FOM}
-structure is a tuple
\mathfrak{M}=(\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i}))

consisting of interpretations
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s})
and
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n})
of the sorts as nonempty sets,
and interpretations

\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N})\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s})\times\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n})
and

\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E})\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n})\times\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s})

and
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i})\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s})
of the predicates.
Every neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}=(W,\gamma,V)
gives rise
to a
\mathsf{FOM}
-structure
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
with interpretations:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=W

\displaystyle(w,a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N})

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad a\in\gamma(w)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n})

\displaystyle=\bigcup\{\gamma(w)\mid w\in W\}

\displaystyle(a,w)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E})

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad w\in a

\displaystyle w\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i})

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad w\in V(p_{i})

In the converse direction, a
\mathsf{FOM}
-structure

\mathfrak{M}=(\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E}),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i}))

yields a neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}=(\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s}),\gamma,V)
,
where
V(p_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i})
and

\gamma(w)=\big\{\{y\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s})\mid a\mathsf{E}y\}\mid w\mathsf{N}a\big\}.

2.3 Definition
.

The standard translation
\st_{x}:\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}\to\mathsf{FOM}
is defined recursively by

\displaystyle\st_{x}(p_{i})

\displaystyle=P_{i}x

\displaystyle\st_{x}(\bot)

\displaystyle=(x\neq x)

\displaystyle\st_{x}(\varphi\to\psi)

\displaystyle=\st_{x}(\varphi)\to\st_{x}(\psi)

\displaystyle\st_{x}(\varphi\wedge\psi)

\displaystyle=\st_{x}(\varphi)\wedge\st_{x}(\psi)

\displaystyle\st_{x}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi)

\displaystyle=(\exists a)(x\mathsf{N}a\wedge(\forall y)(a\mathsf{E}y\to\st_{y}(\varphi)))

\displaystyle\st_{x}(\varphi\vee\psi)

\displaystyle=\st_{x}(\varphi)\vee\st_{x}(\psi)

\displaystyle\st_{x}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi)

\displaystyle=(\forall a)(x\mathsf{N}a\to(\exists y)(a\mathsf{E}y\wedge\st_{y}(\varphi)))

Then we have:

2.4 Proposition
.

For all
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
, neighbourhood models
\mathcal{M}
and

\mathsf{FOM}
-structures
\mathfrak{N}
:

\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M}^{\circ}\models\st_{x}(\varphi)[w],\quad\quad\mathfrak{N}^{\bullet},w\Vdash\varphi\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{N}\models\st_{x}(\varphi)[w].

Therefore, we can view the classical monotone modal logic
\mathsf{M}

as the set of
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
-formulas whose standard translation
is derivable in
\mathsf{FOM}
.

2.2  Intuitionistic first-order logic

Let
\mathsf{IFOM}
be the intuitionistic first-order logic of the same signature
as
\mathsf{FOM}
.
Then an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure is a tuple
\mathfrak{M}=(W,\leq,\mathcal{I})
consisting of
a partially ordered set
(W,\leq)
together
with an interpretation function
\mathcal{I}
that assigns to each world
w\in W

sets
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
and
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)
, and relations

\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w)\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)\times\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)
and

\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w)\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)\times\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
and

\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w)\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
,
such that
w\leq w^{\prime}
implies
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{X},w)\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{X},w^{\prime})

for all
\mathsf{X}\in\{\mathsf{s},\mathsf{n},\mathsf{N},\mathsf{E},\mathsf{P}_{i}\}
.
In other words, for each
w\in W
we get a (classical)
\mathsf{FOM}
-structure

(\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w),\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w))
, and these
increase with respect to inclusion as we go up along the intuitionistic
accessibility relation.

If
\alpha(x)
is a first-order formula with free variable
x
,
and
d
is a domain element of some first-order structure
\mathfrak{M}
,
then we denote by
\alpha(x)[d]
the formula obtained from interpreting

x
as
d
.

2.5 Example
.

The following diagram depicts an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure:
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w_{1})
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w_{1})
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w_{2})
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w_{2})
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w_{3})
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w_{3})
w_{1}
d_{1}
d_{2}
a_{1}
w_{2}
d_{1}
d_{2}
d_{3}
a_{1}
w_{3}
d_{1}
d_{2}
d_{3}
d_{4}
a_{1}
a_{2}
\leq
\leq

Suppose
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w_{2})=\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w_{2})=\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w_{3})=\{d_{2}\}
.
Then
w_{1}\models(\forall a)(d_{1}\mathsf{N}a\to(\exists y)(a\mathsf{E}y\wedge\mathsf{P}_{i}y))
:
the intuitionistic interpretation of the universal quantifier requires
us to verify for each successor
v
of
w_{1}
that
x\mathsf{N}a
implies the existence
of some
y
such that
a\mathsf{E}y
and
\mathsf{P}_{i}y
.
And this is indeed the case, because at each “level”
w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}
,
the state
d_{1}
is only connected to neighbourhood
a_{1}
, and in each case

d_{2}
is a suitable
y
.
Since the given formula is the standard translation of

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptp_{i}
with
x
interpreted as
d_{1}
, we find that

w_{1}\models\st_{x}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108ptp_{i})[d_{1}]
.

The standard translation
\st_{x}
from Definition
2.3
can be viewed
as a translation from
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

into

.

-consecutions, i.e. expressions of the form

, as follows.

2.6 Definition
.

The logic
\mathsf{IM}
consists of all
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
-consecutions
\Gamma\vdash\varphi
such that
for every
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure
\mathfrak{M}
, every world
w
in
\mathfrak{M}

and every
d\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
,

\mathfrak{M},w\models\st_{x}(\Gamma)[d]
implies
\mathfrak{M},w\models\st_{x}(\varphi)[d]
.
We write
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM}}\varphi
if a consecution
\Gamma\vdash\varphi

is in
\mathsf{IM}
.

Intuitively, this states that
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM}}\varphi

if
\st_{x}(\Gamma)\models\st_{x}(\varphi)
in all
pointed
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures, where the point indicates the interpretation of
x
.
We can use the first-order structures for
\mathsf{IFOM}
as models
for
\mathsf{IM}
. This is in analogy with IL-models for the intuitionistic
modal logic
\mathsf{IK}

[
40
, Section 5.2]
.

2.7 Definition
.

Let

\mathfrak{M}=(W,\leq,\mathcal{I})

be an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure.
Formulas from
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
can be interpreted in pairs of the form
(w,x)
,
where
w\in W
and
x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
, as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models p_{i}

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\bot

never

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\varphi\wedge\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\varphi\text{ and }\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\psi

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\varphi\vee\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\varphi\text{ or }\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\psi

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }w^{\prime}\geq w,\;\;\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime},x\models\varphi\text{ implies }\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime},x\models\psi

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{there exists }a\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)\text{ such that }(x,a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w)\text{ and such that}

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad\text{iff}\quad}\text{for all }w^{\prime}\geq w\text{ and all }x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w^{\prime}),\;(a,x^{\prime})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w^{\prime})\text{ implies }\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\models\varphi

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }w^{\prime}\geq w\text{ and all }a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w^{\prime}),\text{ if }(x,a^{\prime})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w^{\prime})

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad\text{iff}\quad}\text{ then there exists }y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w^{\prime})\text{ s.t. }(a^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w^{\prime})\text{ and }\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime},y^{\prime}\models\varphi

For
\Gamma\subseteq\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
, we write
\mathfrak{M},w,x\Vdash\Gamma

if
\mathfrak{M},w,x\Vdash\psi
for all
\psi\in\Gamma
.
Furthermore, we write
\Gamma\models\varphi
if
\mathfrak{M},w,x\Vdash\Gamma

implies
\mathfrak{M},w,x\Vdash\varphi
, for every
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure

\mathfrak{M}=(W,\leq,\mathcal{I})
and
every
w\in W
and
x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
.

Then by construction:

2.8 Theorem
.

For all

,
we have
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM}}\varphi
if and only if
\Gamma\models\varphi
.

2.3  Calculi for intuitionistic monotone modal logics

We define various intuitionistic monotone modal logics
via generalised Hilbert calculi
(see e.g.
[
43
, Section 2.4]
or
[
39
, Section 4.1]
).
This allows us to define a consequence relation (i.e. a set consecutions

\Gamma\vdash\varphi
)
using axioms and rules, while at the same time being careful about
the shape of
\Gamma
allowed in the rules, avoiding confusions such as
those pointed out in
[
27
]
.
(For example, the premises of the modus ponens rule allow for any
\Gamma
,
while those of the monotonicity rules require
\Gamma=\emptyset
.)

2.9 Definition
.

Let
\operatorname{Ax}\subseteq\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

be a set of axioms.
Let

be the set of substitution instances of axioms in

together with all substitution instances of an

satisfies the deduction theorem.

2.10 Theorem
.

Let
\Gamma\cup\{\varphi,\psi\}\subseteq\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

3  Intuitionistic neighbourhood semantics

The interpretation of
from Definition
2.7
relies
on what worlds
v
are in some neighbourhood
a
(that is, on

a\mathsf{E}v
), quantified over intuitionistic successors of
w
.
In particular, this elementhood can change when moving along the intuitionistic
successors of
w
.
In other words, we may have
w\leq w^{\prime}
and
v\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w^{\prime})
while
v\notin\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w)
.
This suggests that when moving from a classical to an intuitionistic setting,
we need to adapt our notion of a neighbourhood. Rather than a subset of
worlds, a neighbourhood can change when moving
along the intuitionistic accessibility relation.
This gives rise to the notion of an
intuitionistic neighbourhood
.
An intuitionistic neighbourhood model then becomes an intuitionistic Kripke
frame together with a set of intuitionistic neighbourhoods and a valuation.

Neighbourhoods of a world can be viewed as sets of evidence.
Intuitionistic neighbourhoods then expresses the idea that evidence can
change when moving along the intuitionistic accessibility relation.
Necessity of
\varphi
, i.e. truth of
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi
, at a world
w
then follows
from finding evidence for
\varphi
at all successors of
w
.
Similarly, possibility of
\varphi
has to be shown at all successors.

In this section we prove that such intuitionistic neighbourhood models
provide another sound and complete semantics for
\mathsf{IM}

by constructing truth-preserving translations between intuitionistic neighbourhood
models and
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures.
While translating
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures to intuitionistic neighbourhood models is
straightforward (Section
3.1
),
the converse direction proceeds in three steps:
given an intuitionistic neighbourhood model, we first turn it into
a
coherent
model (Section
3.2
),
then into a
Cartesian
one (Section
3.4
),
and we show that the coherent and Cartesian models are those
isomorphic to intuitionistic neighbourhood models arising from
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures(Section
3.3
).

Finally, in Section
3.5
we use a canonical model construction
to prove that
\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}
is complete with respect to the class of intuitionistic
neighbourhood models, which entials that the generalised Hilbert calculus
defining
\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}
axiomatises
\mathsf{IM}
.

3.1  Intuitionistic neighbourhood frames

If
(W,\leq)
is an intuitionistic Kripke frame and
a\subseteq W
,
then we define
{\uparrow}a=\{v\in W\mid w\leq v\text{ for some }w\in a\}
.
An
upset
is a subset
a\subseteq W
such that
a={\uparrow}a
,
and we denote the set of upsets of
(W,\leq)
by
\up(W,\leq)
.
In its simplest form, we can define an intuitionistic neighbourhood as follows.

3.1 Definition
.

Let
(W,\leq)
be an intuitionistic Kripke frame.
An
intuitionistic neighbourhood
is a partial function

a:W\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}W
such that its domain

\dom(a):=\{w\in W\mid a(w)\text{ is defined}\}
is
an upset of
(W,\leq)
.

Intuitionistic neighbourhood frames and models then arise from equipping
an intuitionistic Kripke frame or model with a set of intuitionistic neighbourhoods.

3.2 Definition
.

An
intuitionistic neighbourhood frame
is a tuple

(W,\leq,N)
consisting of an intuitionistic Kripke frame
(W,\leq)

and a collection
N
of intuitionistic neighbourhoods.
We say that
a\in N
is a neighbourhood of
w\in W
if

w\in\dom(a)
, and we write
N_{w}
for the collection of neighbourhoods
of
w
.

An
intuitionistic neighbourhood model
is a tuple

\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
where
(W,\leq,N)
is an intuitionistic
neighbourhood frame and
V:\Prop\to\up(W,\leq)
is a valuation
of the proposition letters.
The interpretation of
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
-formulas in a world
w
of
\mathcal{M}

is defined recursively via:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\bot

never

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash p

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad w\in V(p)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\wedge\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\text{ and }\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\psi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\vee\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\text{ or }\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\psi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }v\geq w,\;\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi\text{ implies }\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\psi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{there exists }a\in N_{w}\text{ such that for all }w^{\prime}\geq w,\;\;v\in a(w^{\prime})\text{ implies }\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi

\displaystyle\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }w^{\prime}\geq w\text{ and all }a\in N_{w^{\prime}}\text{ there exists }v\in a(w^{\prime})\text{ such that }\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi

If

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
is a set of formulas, then we write

\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\Gamma
if
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\psi
for all
\psi\in\Gamma
.
We denote the
truth set
of
\varphi
in
\mathcal{M}
by

\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket^{\mathcal{M}}:=\{w\in W\mid\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\}
.
A model
\mathcal{M}

validates
a consecution
\Gamma\vdash\varphi

if any world that satisfies all formulas in
\Gamma
also satisfies
\varphi
,
and a frame
\mathcal{F}=(W,\leq,N)
validates
\Gamma\vdash\varphi

if every model of the form
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
validates it.
Lastly, we say that
\varphi
is a
semantic consequence
of
\Gamma

over the class of intuitionistic neighbourhood models if every intuitionistic
neighbourhood model validates
\Gamma\vdash\varphi
, and we denote this
by
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
.

The next lemma extends the usual persistence property for intuitionistic
logic (see e.g.
[
10
, Proposition 2.1]
) to the modal setting.
It can be proven by a routine induction on the structure of
\varphi
.

3.3 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.
For all formulas
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
and all worlds
w,v\in W
,
if
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi
and
w\leq v
then
\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi
.

While we do not need bounded morphisms,
for future reference we define isomorphisms between models.

3.4 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=(W^{\prime},\leq^{\prime},N^{\prime},V^{\prime})
be two
intuitionistic Kripke models.
An
isomorphism
from
\mathcal{M}
to
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
consists of a pair

(\alpha,\nu)
of bijections
\alpha:W\to W^{\prime}
and
\nu:N\to N^{\prime}

such that for all
w,v\in W
,
a\in N
and
u\in\dom(a)
:

(I
1
)

w\leq v
if and only if
\alpha(w)\leq^{\prime}\alpha(v)

(I
2
)

w\in\dom(a)
if and only if
\alpha(w)\in\dom(\nu(a))

(I
3
)

w\in a(u)
if and only if
\alpha(w)\in(\nu(a))(\alpha(u))

(I
4
)

w\in V(p_{i})
if and only if
\alpha(w)\in V^{\prime}(p_{i})

3.5 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=(W^{\prime},\leq^{\prime},N^{\prime},V^{\prime})
be two
intuitionistic Kripke models
and
(\alpha,\nu):\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
an isomorphism.
Then for all
w\in W
and
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
,
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi

if and only if
\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\alpha(w)\Vdash\varphi
.

Every
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure gives rise to an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

3.6 Definition
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}=(W,\leq,\mathcal{I})
be
an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure.
Then we define the intuitionistic Kripke frame
(W^{\bullet},\leqq)

by

\displaystyle W^{\bullet}=\{\langle w,x\rangle\mid w\in W,x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)\},

\displaystyle\langle w,x\rangle\leqq\langle w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\rangle\quad\text{iff}\quad w\leq w^{\prime}\text{ and }x=x^{\prime}

For each
a\in\bigcup\{\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)\mid w\in W\}
, define an intuitionistic
neighbourhood
a^{\bullet}:W^{\bullet}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}(W^{\bullet})

by setting

a^{\bullet}(\langle w,x\rangle)=\{\langle w,y\rangle\in W^{\bullet}\mid(a,y)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},w)\}

if
a\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)
and
(x,a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w)
,
and leaving
a^{\bullet}
undefined otherwise.
Let
N^{\bullet}=\{a^{\bullet}\mid a\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)\text{ for some }w\in W\}
.
Then
(W^{\bullet},\leqq,N^{\bullet})
is an intuitionistic neighbourhood frame.
Finally, define the valuation
V^{\bullet}
by

V^{\bullet}(p_{i})=\{\langle w,x\rangle\in W^{\bullet}\mid x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w)\}.

and let
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}=(W^{\bullet},\leqq,N^{\bullet},V^{\bullet})
be the
resulting model.

3.7 Lemma
.

If
\mathfrak{M}
is a
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure then
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
is an
intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

Proof.

Since
\leq
is a partial order, so is
\leqq
.
The domain of each
a^{\bullet}
is an upset of
(W^{\bullet},\leqq)
because

w\leq w^{\prime}
implies
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w^{\prime})
.
To see that the valuation sends propositions to upsets,
suppose
\langle w,x\rangle\in V^{\bullet}(p_{i})
and

\langle w,x\rangle\leqq\langle w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\rangle
.
Then
w\leq w^{\prime}
and
x=x^{\prime}
and it follows from the fact that

\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w)\subseteq\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w^{\prime})
that
\langle w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\rangle\in V^{\bullet}(p_{i})
.
∎

3.8 Proposition
.

For every
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures

\mathfrak{M}=(W,\leq,\mathcal{I})

and all
w\in W
,
x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},w)
and
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
,

\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\varphi\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet},\langle w,x\rangle\Vdash\varphi.

Proof.

We use induction on the structure of
\varphi
.
If
\varphi=\bot
then the proposition follows from the fact that
\bot

is never satisfied. If
\varphi=p_{i}\in\Prop
, then we have

\mathfrak{M},w,x\models p_{i}\quad\text{iff}\quad x\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},w)\quad\text{iff}\quad\langle w,x\rangle\in V^{\bullet}(p_{i})\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet},\langle w,x\rangle\Vdash p_{i}.

The inductive steps for
\varphi=(\psi\wedge\chi)
and
\varphi=(\psi\vee\chi)

are straightforward. Suppose
\varphi=\psi\to\chi
. Then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M},w,x\Vdash\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }w^{\prime}\geq w,\;\;\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime},x\Vdash\psi\text{ implies }\mathfrak{M},w^{\prime},x\Vdash\chi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all }\langle w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\rangle\geqq\langle w,x\rangle,\;\;\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet},\langle w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\rangle\Vdash\psi\text{ implies }\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet},\langle w^{\prime},x^{\prime}\rangle\Vdash\chi

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet},\langle w,x\rangle\Vdash\psi\to\chi.

Here the second “iff” uses the definition of
\geqq
and the induction
hypothesis.

Next suppose
\varphi=\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
.
If
\mathfrak{M},w,x\models\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
then there exists an

such that

and

.
This
a
gives rise to a neighbourhood
a^{\bullet}
of

\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
witnessing the truth of

\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet},\langle w,x\rangle\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
.
Conversely, the existence of a suitable neighbourhood

witnessing that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

yields
