\varphi=\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
also follows from the fact that neighbourhoods
of
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
correspond precisely to elements of sort
\mathsf{n}
in
\mathfrak{M}
.
∎

3.9 Theorem

(Soundness)
.

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}}\varphi
then
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi

and
\Gamma\models\varphi
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
.
Proposition
3.8
then implies
\Gamma\models\varphi
.
We use induction on a proof
\delta
of
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}}\varphi
.
This gives rise to the following five cases.

The last rule in
\delta
is
(\mathsf{El})
.

In this case we have
\varphi\in\Gamma
. Clearly, this implies that any world
in any intuitionistic neighbourhood model that satisfies
\Gamma
also
satisfies
\varphi
, so
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
.

The last rule in
\delta
is
(\mathsf{Ax})
.

Since
\Gamma
is arbitrary, we need to prove that the substitution
instances of all axioms are valid on all intuitionistic neighbourhood models.
For the axioms of intuitionistic logic, this is well known.

To prove validity of
(\mathsf{neg_{a}})
, let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be any
intuitionistic neighbourhood model
and suppose
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\wedge\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\neg\varphi
.
Then
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi
, so there exists an intuitionistic
neighbourhood
a\in N
such that
w\in\dom(a)
and

a(w^{\prime})\subseteq\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket^{\mathcal{M}}
for all
w^{\prime}\geq w
.
In particular,
a(w)\subseteq\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket^{\mathcal{M}}
.
But we also have
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\neg\varphi
, which
implies that we can find some
v\in a(w)
such that
\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\neg\varphi
.
So
\mathcal{M},v\Vdash\varphi
and
\mathcal{M},v\not\Vdash\varphi
, a contradiction.
We conclude that no world satisfies
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\wedge\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

,
hence
(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\wedge\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\neg\varphi)\to\bot
is valid.
Since
\mathcal{M}
is arbitrary, this proves

\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\wedge\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

.

We argue similarly for
(\mathsf{I_{\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt}})
.
Suppose
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\top\to\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

.
To see that
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

, let

. Then

. Since

.

. Therefore

.

The last rule in
\delta
is
(\mathsf{MP})
.

By induction
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\psi\to\varphi

and
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\psi
. So any world
w
in any model
\mathcal{M}

that satisfies
\Gamma
, also satisfies
\psi\to\varphi
and
\varphi
.
By definition we then get
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi
.
Therefore
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
.

The last rule in
\delta
is
(\mathsf{Mon_{\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt}})
or
(\mathsf{Mon_{\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

.

In this case

must be of the form

,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\}
,
and by induction
\emptyset\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\psi\to\chi
.
Then we must have
\llbracket\psi\rrbracket^{\mathcal{M}}\subseteq\llbracket\chi\rrbracket^{\mathcal{M}}

for any model
\mathcal{M}
. Using this, it follows immediately from the
definition of the interpretations of
and
that

\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\Star\psi
implies
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\Star\chi

for any intuitionistic neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}
and world
w
.
∎

3.2  Coherent intuitionistic neighbourhoods

If we think of an intuitionistic Kripke frame as a collection of information
states which gain increasingly more information, and of intuitionistic
neighbourhoods as evidence, then one would expect that evidence does not
get lost when progressing along the intuitionistic accessibility relation.
If anything, evidence should get more specific. Thus, a condition
we may like to put on our intuitionistic neighbourhoods is

(N
1
)

if
w\leq w^{\prime}
and
v\in a(w)
then there exists a
v^{\prime}\in W

such that
v^{\prime}\in a(w^{\prime})
such that
v\leq v^{\prime}
.

Reasoning in an ideal world, we could also hope that more specific evidence
will eventually be found. That is,

(N
2
)

if
v\in a(w)
and
v\leq v^{\prime}
then there exists a
w^{\prime}\in W

such that
w\leq w^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}\in a(w^{\prime})
.

3.10 Definition
.

Let
(W,\leq)
be an intuitionistic Kripke frame. An intuitionistic
neighbourhood
a:W\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}(W)
is called
coherent

if it satisfies (
N1
) and (
N2
).
An intuitionistic neighbourhood frame or model is called coherent if all its
intuitionistic neighbourhoods are coherent.
We write
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{coh}}\varphi
if the consecution
\Gamma\vdash\varphi

is valid in all coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood models.

w
v
w^{\prime}
v^{\prime}
(
N1
)
a
a
w
v
w^{\prime}
v^{\prime}
(
N2
)
a
a

Figure 1:
A depiction of the coherence conditions.
Here the arrows indicate the intuitionistic
accessibility relation and
w
v
a
indicates that
v\in a(w)
.

Both coherence conditions are satisfied in intuitionistic Kripke models arising
from
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures via Definition
3.6
,
and in fact that is our motivation to investigate them further.
Moreover, it turns out that we can modify any intuitionistic neighbourhood
model into one satisfying (
N1
) and (
N2
) without
affecting the truth of formulas.
Intuitively, this is achieved by introducing copies of neighbourhoods
relative to the worlds in their domain in such a way that they satisfy (
N1
).
Second, we unravel maximal worlds,
i.e. the worlds that have no other worlds strictly above them,
in order to satisfy (
N2
).
Interestingly, the canonical model construction we give in
Section
3.5
also requires an unravelling of
maximal points.

3.11 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.
Let

W^{\mathrm{coh}}:=\{(w,0)\in W\times\mathbb{N}\mid w\in W,w\text{ not maximal}\}\cup\{(w,n)\in W\times\mathbb{N}\mid w\in W,w\text{ is maximal}\}.

Define
\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}
on
W^{\mathrm{coh}}
by
(w,n)\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}(v,m)
if

w\leq v
and
n\leq m
.

For any
w\in W
and
a\in N
such that
w\in\dom(a)
,
define
a(w)^{\mathrm{coh}}:=\{(u,k)\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}\mid u\in a(w)\}
.
Then, for
a\in N
and
(v,m)\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}
we define the intuitionistic
neighbourhood
a_{v,m}
to be the partial function with domain

\{(w,n)\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}\mid(v,m)\leq(w,n)\}
given by

a_{v,m}(w,n)=\begin{cases}a(w)^{\mathrm{coh}}&\text{if }(v,m)=(w,n)\\
{\uparrow}(\textstyle\bigcup\{a(u)^{\mathrm{coh}}\mid v\leq u\})&\text{if }(v,m)<^{\mathrm{coh}}(w,n)\end{cases}

Let
N^{\mathrm{coh}}=\{a_{v,m}\mid a\in N,(v,m)\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}\}
.
Finally, define a valuation by
V^{\mathrm{coh}}(p_{i})=\{(w,n)\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}\mid w\in V(p_{i})\}
and let
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{coh}}:=(W^{\mathrm{coh}},\leq^{\mathrm{coh}},N^{\mathrm{coh}},V^{\mathrm{coh}})
.

3.12 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.
Then
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{coh}}
is a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

Proof.

We need to verify that
a_{v,m}
is a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood,
for every
a\in N
and
(v,m)\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}
.
So let
a_{v,m}\in N^{\mathrm{coh}}
.
Then its domain is an upset by definition.
Item (
N1
) follows from the fact that if

(w,n)\in\dom(a_{v,m})
and
(w,n)\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})
, then

a_{v,m}(w,n)\subseteq a_{v,m}(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})
by construction.
For (
N2
), suppose
(u,k)\in a_{v,m}(w,n)
and
(u,k)\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}(u^{\prime},k^{\prime})
.
By construction
(w,n)
has a successor
(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})
and

a_{v,m}(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})
contains
(u,k)
and is upwards closed, hence contains
(u^{\prime},k^{\prime})
.
∎

.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)

.
Let
(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})\in W^{\mathrm{coh}}
and
a_{v,m}\in N^{\mathrm{coh}}
be
such that
(w,n)\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})

and
(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})\in\dom(a_{v,m})
. We need to find a world in

a_{v,m}(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})
that satisfies
\psi
.
By definition of
\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}
and
a_{v,m}
we get
w\leq w^{\prime}
and
v\leq w^{\prime}
and

w^{\prime}\in\dom(a)
, so we can find some
u\in a(w^{\prime})
such that
\mathcal{M},u\Vdash\psi
.
By induction this means that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{coh}},(u,0)\Vdash\psi
.
Further,
(u,0)\in a(w^{\prime})^{\mathrm{coh}}\subseteq a_{v,m}(w^{\prime},n^{\prime})
, so we
have found the desired world.

For the converse we reason by contrapositive, so we assume

\mathcal{M},w\not\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
. Then there exists a

w^{\prime}\geq w
and a neighbourhood
a
such that for all
u\in a(w^{\prime})
we
have
\mathcal{M},u\not\Vdash\psi
. The induction hypothesis then implies
that for all
(u,k)\in a(w^{\prime})^{\mathrm{coh}}
we have

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{coh}},(u,k)\not\Vdash\psi
.
Furthermore, we have
(w^{\prime},n)\in\dom(a_{w^{\prime},n})
and

a_{w^{\prime},n}(w^{\prime},n)=a(w^{\prime})^{\mathrm{coh}}
, so using the fact that

(w,n)\leq^{\mathrm{coh}}(w^{\prime},n)
we find
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{coh}},(w,n)\not\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
.
∎

3.14 Theorem
.

We have
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
if and only if
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{coh}}\varphi
.

Proof.

The direction from left to right follows from the fact that every
coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood model is in particular an intuitionistic
neighbourhood model. The direction from right to left follows from
Proposition
3.13
.
∎

3.15 Remark
.

An interesting situation arises when we restrict
N
in an intuitionistic
neighbourhood frame
(W,\leq N)
to having a single neighbourhood
a
.
If
\dom(a)=W
, then the frame corresponds to a frame for Wijesekera’s
constructive logic
\mathsf{WK}

[
44
,
45
]
, and the interpretation
of the modal operators corresponds to that for
\mathsf{WK}
.
However, if we additionally assume that
a
is coherent, then we obtain a frame or model
for
\mathsf{IK}

[
40
]
(see also Appendix
A
).
So while the coherence condition does not entail any additional valid
consecutions when working with intuitionistic neighbourhood semantics,
it does when we restrict to a setting with a single neighbourhood,
because we know that
\mathsf{IK}
proves strictly more theorems than
\mathsf{WK}
.

3.3  Cartesian intuitionistic neighbourhood models

We have seen that every first-order structure for

\mathsf{IFOM}
gives rise to an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.
In this section we characterise precisely the intuitionistic neighbourhood
models arising from an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure (up to isomorphism).
This gives a condition on the underlying frames.
In analogy to the normal modal case (see
[
21
, Section 3]

and
[
40
, Section 8.1.1]
),
we call such intuitionistic neighbourhood frames Cartesian.

3.16 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be an intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

1.

Define a binary relation
R
on
W
by letting
wRv
if there exists a
neighbourhood
a
such that
v\in a(w)
.
We write
R^{\sim}
for the equivalence closure of
R
.
Further, we write
\leq^{\sim}
for the equivalence closure of
\leq
.

2.

\mathcal{M}
(or its underlying frame) is called

R^{\sim}
-Cartesian
if for all
w,v\in W
,

w\leq^{\sim}vR^{\sim}w
implies
w=v
.

3.

\mathcal{M}
(or its underlying frame) is called

N-Cartesian
or
neighbourhood Cartesian

if
wR^{\sim}v
and
w,v\in\dom(a)
implies
a(w)=a(v)
,
for all
w,v\in W
and
a\in N
.

4.

We say that
\mathcal{M}
is
Cartesian
if it is both

R^{\sim}
-Cartesian and N-Cartesian.

We derive some basic properties of
R^{\sim}
and of Cartesian models.

3.17 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be a coherent and Cartesian
intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

1.

If
wR^{\sim}v\leq u
then there exists an
s\in W
such that

w\leq sR^{\sim}u
.

2.

If
\mathcal{M}
is Cartesian, then
wR^{\sim}v\leq^{\sim}u

and
wR^{\sim}v^{\prime}\leq^{\sim}u
implies
v=v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The first item follows from repeated application of (
N1
) and (
N2
).
For the second item, suppose
wR^{\sim}v\leq^{\sim}u

and
wR^{\sim}v^{\prime}\leq^{\sim}u
.
Then we have
v\leq^{\sim}v^{\prime}R^{\sim}v
, hence if
\mathcal{M}
is
Cartesian we find
v=v^{\prime}
.
∎

w
v
w
R^{\sim}
-Cartesian
w
v
s
u
Lemma
3.17
(
1
)
w
v
v^{\prime}
u
Lemma
3.17
(
2
)

Figure 2:
Depictions of the
R^{\sim}
-Cartesian property and Lemma
3.17
.
In this diagram, and other diagrams in this section, we draw

w
v
a
if
wR^{\sim}v
, and

w
v
if
w\leq^{\sim}v
.

We already observed that intuitionistic neighbourhood models coming
from
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures are coherent. We can easily verify that they
are also Cartesian.

3.18 Lemma
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure.
Then
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
is a Cartesian intuitionistic neighbourhood model.

Proof.

Let
\langle w,x\rangle
and
\langle v,y\rangle
be elements
of
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
.
By definition,
\langle w,x\rangle\leqq\langle v,y\rangle

implies
x=y
. As a consequence,

\langle w,x\rangle\leqq^{\sim}\langle v,y\rangle
also implies
x=y
.
On the other hand, if
a\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},w)
then

\langle v,y\rangle\in a^{\bullet}(\langle w,x\rangle)
implies
w=v
.
This entails that

\langle w,x\rangle R^{\sim}\langle v,y\rangle
implies
w=v
.

Combining the two observations above immediately gives that

\langle w,x\rangle\leqq^{\sim}\langle v,y\rangle R^{\sim}\langle w,x\rangle

implies
w=v
and
x=y
, so that
\langle w,x\rangle=\langle v,y\rangle

and
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
is
R^{\sim}
-Cartesian.
Second, suppose
\langle w,x\rangle R^{\sim}\langle v,y\rangle
.
Then
w=v
, so we assume
\langle w,x\rangle R^{\sim}\langle w,y\rangle
.
If both
\langle w,x\rangle\in\dom(a^{\bullet})

and
\langle w,y\rangle\in\dom(a^{\bullet})
,
then we have
(x,a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w)
and
(y,a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},w)
.
But then it follows from the definition of
a^{\bullet}
that

a^{\bullet}(\langle w,x\rangle)=a^{\bullet}(\langle w,y\rangle)
,
so
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
is N-Cartesian.
∎

The remainder of this section focusses on showing that every
coherent and Cartesian intuitionistic neighbourhood model is isomorphic to an
intuitionistic neighbourhood model of the form
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
,
for some
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure
\mathfrak{M}
. To this end, we fix a
coherent and Cartesian intuitionistic neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
.
Denote the equivalence class of
w\in W
with respect to
R^{\sim}

by
\bar{w}
, and its equivalence class with respect to
\leq^{\sim}

by
\tilde{w}
.

3.19 Definition
.

Let
N_{triv}=\{a\in N\mid\dom(a)=\emptyset\}
be the collection
of intuitionistic neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{M}
whose domain is empty.
Define

\displaystyle\overline{W}

\displaystyle=W/{R^{\sim}}=\{\bar{w}\mid w\in W\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},\bar{w})

\displaystyle=\{\tilde{v}\mid wR^{\sim}w^{\prime}\leq^{\sim}v\text{ for some }w^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},\bar{w})

\displaystyle=\{a\in N\mid wR^{\sim}x\text{ and }x\in\dom(a)\text{ for some }x\in W\}\cup N_{triv}

Define a relation
{\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}}\subseteq\overline{W}\times\overline{W}
, and
interpretations of the predicates by:

\displaystyle\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{w}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{there exists $v^{\prime}$ such that }w\leq v^{\prime}R^{\sim}w^{\prime}

\displaystyle(\tilde{x},a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},\bar{u})

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{for all $w$ we have: }uR^{\sim}w\leq^{\sim}x\text{ implies }w\in\dom(a)

\displaystyle(a,\tilde{x})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},\bar{u})

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{there exist }y,z\text{ such that }uR^{\sim}y\text{ and }z\in a(y)\text{ and }z\leq^{\sim}x

\displaystyle\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i},\bar{u})

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad wR^{\sim}y\leq^{\sim}x\text{ implies }y\in V(p_{i})

Lastly, let
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}:=(\overline{W},\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}},\mathcal{I})
.

We verify that this definition gives rise to a
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure.

3.20 Lemma
.

The tuple

\mathcal{M}^{\circ}=(\overline{W},\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}},\mathcal{I})

is an
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure.

Proof.

\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}
is a partial order on
\overline{W}
.

The relation
\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}
is reflexive because
\leq
and
R^{\sim}
are.
For transitivity, suppose
\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{v}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{u}
.
Then there are
v^{\prime},u^{\prime}
such that
w\leq v^{\prime}R^{\sim}v\leq u^{\prime}R^{\sim}u
.
Now we can use Lemma
3.17
(
1
)
to find
u^{\prime\prime}
such that
v^{\prime}\leq u^{\prime}R^{\sim}u^{\prime}
.
This implies
w\leq u^{\prime\prime}R^{\sim}u
, so that
\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{u}
.
In a picture:

w
v^{\prime}

u^{\prime}
u
u^{\prime\prime}

For antisymmetry, suppose
\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{v}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{w}
.
Then there exist
v^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime}
such that
w\leq v^{\prime}R^{\sim}v\leq w^{\prime}R^{\sim}w
.
Using Lemma
3.17
(

)
again we get
w^{\prime\prime}
such that
v^{\prime}\leq w^{\prime\prime}R^{\sim}w^{\prime}
.
But this means that
w\leq w^{\prime\prime}R^{\sim}w
, so we can use the Cartesian

\mathcal{M}
to obtain
w^{\prime\prime}=w
.
We then get
v^{\prime}=w
because
\leq
is antisymmetric.
This implies
wR^{\sim}v
, hence
\bar{w}=\bar{v}
.

The state domains are increasing.

Suppose
\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{v}

.

wR^{\sim}s\leq^{\sim}u
. We can use

(

) to find

The neighbourhood domains are increasing.

.

) we can find

,

between

is unique)
we have

.

3.21 Proposition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be a coherent and Cartesian intuitionistic
neighbourhood model
and
(\mathcal{M}^{\circ})^{\bullet}=(\overline{W}^{\bullet},\leqq,N^{\bullet},V^{\bullet})

the intuitionistic neighbourhood model induced by
\mathcal{M}^{\circ}
.
Then
\mathcal{M}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\circ})^{\bullet}
.

Proof.

We first establish a bijection between
W
and
\overline{W}^{\bullet}
.
For each
w\in W
we have
wR^{\sim}w\leq^{\sim}w
,
so
\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle\in\overline{W}^{\bullet}
.
Define
\alpha(w):=\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle
.
If
\langle\bar{w},\tilde{x}\rangle\in\overline{W}^{\bullet}
then
by definition we can find some
v\in W
such that
wR^{\sim}v\leq^{\sim}x
,
and by Lemma
3.17
(
2
) this
v
is unique.
So we can define
\beta(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{x}\rangle)
as the unique

v
such that
wR^{\sim}v\leq^{\sim}x
.
Since
w
satisfies
wR^{\sim}w\leq^{\sim}w
we have

\beta(\alpha(w))=\beta(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle)=w
.
Conversely, if
\langle\bar{w},\tilde{x}\rangle\in\overline{W}^{\bullet}

and
v=\beta(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{x}\rangle)
, then

wR^{\sim}v\leq^{\sim}x
so
\bar{w}=\bar{v}
and
\tilde{x}=\tilde{v}
,
so that
\alpha(\beta(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{x}\rangle))=\alpha(v)=\langle\bar{v},\tilde{v}\rangle=\langle\bar{w},\tilde{x}\rangle
.
So
\alpha:W\to\overline{W}^{\bullet}
is a bijection with inverse
\beta
.

Next, let us show that
\alpha:(W,\leq)\to(\overline{W}^{\bullet},\leqq)
is
an order isomorphism.
If
w\leq v
then
\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{v}
and
\tilde{w}=\tilde{v}
,
so
\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle=\langle\bar{w},\tilde{v}\rangle\leqq\langle\bar{v},\tilde{v}\rangle
.
Conversely, suppose
\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle\leqq\langle\bar{v},\tilde{v}\rangle
. Then
\bar{w}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.9pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\leqslant$}}}\bar{v}
and
\tilde{w}=\tilde{v}
.
This means that we can find a
v^{\prime}
such that
w\leq v^{\prime}R^{\sim}v
.
Since
w\leq^{\sim}v
we then get
v\leq^{\sim}v^{\prime}R^{\sim}v
,
so since
\mathcal{M}
is cartesian
v=v^{\prime}
. This implies
w\leq v
.

If
a\in N
and
\dom(a)=\emptyset
then
a
is in the neighbourhood
domain of all worlds in
\overline{W}
, hence
a^{\bullet}\in N^{\bullet}
.
If
\dom(a)\neq\emptyset
then we can find some
w\in W
such that

w\in\dom(a)
, which implies
a\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},\bar{w})
and hence

a^{\bullet}\in N^{\bullet}
.
This yields a map
N\to N^{\bullet}:a\mapsto a^{\bullet}
,
which is a bijection by construction.
To see that
a
and
a^{\bullet}
correspond on their domains, compute

w\in\dom(a)\quad\text{iff}\quad a\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{n},\bar{w})\text{ and }(\tilde{w},a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},\bar{w})\quad\text{iff}\quad\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle\in\dom(a^{\bullet}).

So we have
w\in\dom(a)
if and only if
\alpha(w)\in\dom(a^{\bullet})
.

Next we prove that
v\in a(w)
if and only if
\alpha(v)\in a^{\bullet}(\alpha(w))
.
Suppose
v\in a(w)
.
Then
(\tilde{w},a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},\bar{w})
, because
wR^{\sim}w\leq^{\sim}w
and
w\in\dom(a)
.
Furthermore, we have
wR^{\sim}w
and
v\in a(w)
and
v\leq^{\sim}v
,
so by definition
(a,\tilde{v})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},\bar{w})
.
This then implies
\langle\bar{w},\tilde{v}\rangle\in a^{\bullet}(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle)
. Finally, since
v\in a(w)
we have
wR^{\sim}v
, so

\bar{w}=\bar{v}
, hence
\langle\bar{v},\tilde{v}\rangle\in a^{\bullet}(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle)
.

For the converse, assume
\langle\bar{v},\tilde{v}\rangle\in a^{\bullet}(\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle)
.
Then
\bar{w}=\bar{v}
, and
(\tilde{w},a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},\bar{w})

and
(a,\tilde{v})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},\bar{w})
.
Since
(\tilde{w},a)\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{N},\bar{w})
we have
w\in\dom(a)
,
and
(a,\tilde{v})\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{E},\bar{w})
implies the existence of
y,z\in\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{s},\bar{w})

such that
wR^{\sim}y
and
z\in a(y)
and
z\leq^{\sim}v
.
In a diagram:

w
y
z
v
a

Then
y\in\dom(a)
, so since
wR^{\sim}y
and
\mathcal{M}
is N-Cartesian
we find
z\in a(w)
.
Since
\bar{w}=\bar{v}
, we find
z=\beta(\bar{v},\tilde{v})=v
,
hence
v\in a(w)
.

Lastly, it follows immediately from the definitions of
\mathcal{I}(\mathsf{P}_{i})

and
V^{\bullet}
that
w\in V(p_{i})
if and only if

\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle\in V^{\bullet}(p_{i})
.
We conclude that
\mathcal{M}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\circ})^{\bullet}
.
∎

Now we come to the main theorem of the section.

3.22 Theorem
.

An intuitionistic neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}
is isomorphic to
an intuitionistic neighbourhood model of the form
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
,
for some
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure
\mathfrak{M}
, if and only if
it is coherent and Cartesian.

Proof.

The direction from left to right follows from the fact that every
intuitionistic neighbourhood model of the form
\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
is
coherent and Cartesian.
Conversely, Proposition
3.21
allows us to find a

\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure
\mathfrak{M}
such that

\mathfrak{M}^{\bullet}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}
for each coherent and Cartesian model
\mathcal{M}
.
∎

3.23 Corollary
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be a coherent and Cartesian intuitionistic
neighbourhood model. For every formula
\varphi
and every
w\in W
,

\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M}^{\circ},\bar{w},\tilde{w}\Vdash\varphi.

Proof.

We have
\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\varphi
if and only if

(\mathcal{M}^{\circ})^{\bullet},\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle\Vdash\varphi

because of the isomorphism from Proposition
3.21
,
and
(\mathcal{M}^{\circ})^{\bullet},\langle\bar{w},\tilde{w}\rangle\Vdash\varphi

if and only if
\mathcal{M}^{\circ},\bar{w},\tilde{w}\models\varphi

by Proposition
3.8
.
∎

3.4  Unravelling

In the previous section, we have seen that the class of
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structures
and the class of coherent Cartesian intuitionistic neighbourhood models satisfy
the same consecutions. Therefore
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\models\varphi
.
We prove that the reverse implication also holds by showing that for any
intuitionistic neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}
and world
w
,
we can construct a coherent and Cartesian intuitionistic neighbourhood model

\mathcal{M}^{ur}_{w}
in which there is a world
w^{\prime}
that satisfies precisely
the same
\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
-formulas as
w
.
As a consequence, any consecution that is falsifiable in an intuitionistic
neighbourhood model is falsifiable in a coherent and Cartesian one.
We prove this using an adaptation of the unravelling technique
for Kripke frames, see e.g.
[
4
, Section 2.1]
or
[
10
, Section 3.3]
.

We start by defining paths through an intuitionistic neighbourhood frame or model.

3.24 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{F}=(W,\leq,N)
be an intuitionistic neighbourhood frame.
A
path
through
\mathcal{F}
is a sequence of the form

(w_{0},r_{0},w_{1},r_{1},\ldots,r_{n-1},w_{n})
such that
w_{j}\in W
for all

j\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
, and for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}
either

•

r_{i}={\leq}
and
w_{i}\leq w_{i+1}
; or

•

r_{i}\in N
and
w_{i}\in\dom(r_{i})
and
w_{i+1}\in r_{i}(w_{i})
.

If
\vec{w}=(w_{0},r_{0},w_{1},r_{1},\ldots,r_{n-1},w_{n})
is a path
in
\mathcal{F}
then we let
\first(\vec{w})=w_{0}
and
\last(\vec{w})=w_{n}
,
and we define
\length(\vec{w})=n
.
If
a\in N
and
x\in W
are such that
w_{n}\in\dom(a)
and
x\in a(w_{n})
,
then we write
\vec{w}:a:x
for the
path obtained from appending
a
and
x
to
\vec{w}
.
If
\vec{v}=(v_{0},s_{0},v_{1},s_{1},\ldots,s_{m-1},v_{m})
is another path
and
w_{n}=v_{0}
, then we denote by

\vec{w}\smallfrown\vec{v}
the path obtained from composing
\vec{w}
and

\vec{v}
and identifying
w_{n}
with
v_{0}
.

The path
\vec{w}
is called an
order path
if
r_{i}={\leq}

for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}
,
and a
neighbourhood path
if
r_{i}\in N
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}
.
Finally, we call
\vec{w}
an
unravelling path
if there exist
an order path
\vec{v}
and a neighbourhood path
\vec{u}
such that

\vec{w}=\vec{v}\smallfrown\vec{u}
.
In this case, we define
\vec{w}_{ord}:=\vec{v}
and
\vec{w}_{nbd}:=\vec{u}
.

3.25 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{F}=(W,\leq,N)
be a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood frame
and let
s\in W
be a world. Then we let
W_{s}^{ur}
denote the set of
unravelling paths starting at
s
.

Let
\vec{w},\vec{v}\in W^{ur}_{s}
and suppose

\vec{w}_{nbd}=(w_{0},a_{0},w_{1},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1},w_{n})

and
\vec{v}_{nbd}=(v_{0},b_{0},v_{1},b_{1},\ldots,b_{m-1},v_{m})
.
Then we let
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
if
n=m
,

a_{i}=b_{i}
for each
i\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}
, and there exist
order-paths
\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{n}
such that

•

\length(\vec{t}_{0})=\ldots=\length(\vec{t}_{n})
; and

•

for each
j\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
,

\first(\vec{t}_{j})=w_{j}
and

\last(\vec{t}_{j})=v_{j}

•

\vec{v}_{ord}=\vec{w}_{ord}\smallfrown\vec{t}_{0}

3.26 Example
.

Consider the intuitionistic neighbourhood frame
(W,\leq,N)
with

W=\{s,t,u,v,w,x\}
, ordered by the reflexive closure of

w\leq v,u\leq t
and
s\leq x
, and with
N=\{a\}
given by

a(v)=\{u,t\}
,
a(u)=\{s\}
and
a(t)=\{x\}
.
This is depicted in Figure
3
.
Consider the unravelling paths

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}=(w,{\leq},v,a,u,a,s)},\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}=(w,{\leq},v,a,t,a,x)},\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vec{p}_{3}=(w,{\leq},v,{\leq},v,a,u,a,s)}.

Then their order parts are

({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}})_{ord}=({\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}})_{ord}=(w,{\leq},v)

and
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vec{p}_{3}})_{ord}=(w,{\leq},v,{\leq},v)
.
Their neighbourhood paths are

({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}})_{nbd}=(v,a,u,a,s)

and
({\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}})_{nbd}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vec{p}_{3}})_{nbd}=(v,a,t,a,x)
.

w
v
u
t
s
x
\vec{p}_{1}
\vec{p}_{2}
\vec{p}_{3}

Figure 3:
The intuitionistic neighbourhood frame from Example
3.26
.
The arrows denote the order
\leq
, and the lines with a dot at the
end denote the neighbourhood relation,
i.e.
v
u
means
u\in a(v)
.

In this example we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}\nleq^{ur}{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}}
:
while we can find paths
\vec{t}_{0},\vec{t}_{1}
and
\vec{t}_{2}

connecting
({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}})_{nbd}
and
({\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}})_{nbd}
pointwise,
these need to have length
>1
because for example

\vec{t}_{2}
needs to be such that
\first(\vec{t}_{2})=s
and
\last(\vec{t}_{2})=x
.
In order to make
\vec{t}_{0}
have length
>1
we need to take

\vec{t}_{0}=(v,{\leq},v)
(and similar if we want to make the length of
\vec{t}_{0}
even longer).
But this implies

({\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}})_{ord}=(w,{\leq},v)\neq(w,{\leq},v,{\leq},v)=(w,{\leq},v)\smallfrown(v,{\leq},v)=({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}})_{ord}\smallfrown\vec{t}_{0}

On the other hand, taking
\vec{t}_{0}=(v,{\leq},v)
,

\vec{t}_{1}=(u,{\leq},t)
and
\vec{t}_{2}=(s,{\leq},x)

shows that
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}\leq^{ur}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vec{p}_{3}}
.

If we would allow the
\vec{t}_{i}
to have different lengths,
then we would get
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}\leq^{ur}{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}}
.
The reason we do not do this is that it would falsify the
R^{\sim}
-Cartesian
property in the unravelling.
We will see in Lemma
3.31
(
4
) that two paths are equivalent
modulo
R^{\sim}
if their order paths coincide, so

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}R^{\sim}{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}}
.
Then, if we were to loosen our definition of
\leq^{ur}
, we would
get
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}\leq^{\sim}{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}}R^{\sim}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\vec{p}_{1}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\vec{p}_{2}}
this violates the
R^{\sim}
-Cartesian property.

3.27 Lemma
.

The pair
(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur})
is an intuitionistic Kripke frame.

Proof.

Reflexivity of
\leq^{ur}
follows from reflexivity of
\leq
,
transitivity of
\leq^{ur}
follows from the definition.
For antisymmetry, suppose
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}\leq^{ur}\vec{w}
.
Then
\vec{w}_{ord}
extends
\vec{v}_{ord}
and
\vec{v}_{ord}
extends

\vec{w}_{ord}
so we must have
\vec{w}_{ord}=\vec{v}_{ord}
.
This implies that the paths
\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{n}
connecting

\vec{w}_{nbd}
and
\vec{v}_{nbd}
, where
n=\length(\vec{w}_{nbd})
,
all have length 1. Then it follows from the definition of
\leq^{ur}
that

\vec{w}_{nbd}=\vec{v}_{nbd}
. Therefore
\vec{w}=\vec{v}
.
∎

We now define coherent neighbourhoods for
(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur})
.

3.28 Definition
.

Let
\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur}
and
a\in N
such that
\last(\vec{w})\in\dom(a)
.
Define the neighbourhood
a_{\vec{w}}:W_{s}^{ur}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}(W_{s}^{ur})

to be the partial function with domain

\dom(a_{\vec{w}})=\{\vec{v}\in W_{s}^{ur}\mid\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}\}

given by

a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{v})=\{\vec{v}:a:x\mid x\in a(\last(\vec{v}))\}.

We verify that these are indeed coherent intuitionistic neighbourhoods.

3.29 Lemma
.

Let
\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur}
and
a\in N
such that
\last(\vec{w})\in\dom(a)
.
Then the partial function
a_{\vec{w}}
is a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood
for
(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur})
.

Proof.

The domain of
a_{\vec{w}}
is an upset by definition,
so it is an intuitionistic neighbourhood.

For (
N1
), suppose
\vec{v}\leq^{ur}\vec{u}

and
(\vec{v}:a:x)\in a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{v})
.
(Note that any element of
a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{v})
must be of this shape.)
By definition of
\leq^{ur}
, there exist order paths

\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{n}
between
\vec{v}_{nbd}
and
\vec{u}_{nbd}
,
where
n=\length(\vec{v}_{nbd})=\length(\vec{u}_{nbd})
.
The definition of
a_{\vec{w}}
yields
x\in a(\last(\vec{v}))
.
Since
a
is an intuitionistic neighbourhood of
\mathcal{F}
,
we can use (
N1
) repeatedly to find some path
\vec{s}
of the
same length as
\vec{t}_{n}
such that
\last(\vec{s})\in a(\last(\vec{u}))
.
Let
y=\last(\vec{s})
. Then
(\vec{u}:a:y)\in a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{u})
,
and
\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{n},\vec{s}
witness that

(\vec{v}:a:x)\leq^{ur}(\vec{u}:a:y)
.
This proves that (
N1
) holds for
a_{\vec{w}}
.

To see that (
N2
) holds, suppose

(\vec{v}:a:x)\in a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{v})

and
(\vec{v}:a:x)\leq^{ur}\vec{u}
.
Then there exists paths
\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{n}
between

(\vec{v}:a:x)_{nbd}
and
\vec{u}_{nbd}
witnessing

(\vec{v}:a:x)\leq^{ur}\vec{u}
.
Furthermore,
\vec{u}
must be of the form
\vec{s}:a:y
.
The order-paths
\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{n-1}
then show that

\vec{v}\leq^{ur}\vec{s}
, and by definition of

a_{\vec{w}}
we have
\vec{u}\in a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{s})
.
So
a_{\vec{w}}
satisfies (
N2
).
∎

3.30 Definition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood model.
Let
N^{ur}=\{a_{\vec{w}}\mid\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur},a\in N\text{ and }\last(\vec{w})\in\dom(a)\}
.
Define a valuation
V^{ur}
for
(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur})
by

V^{ur}(p_{i})=\{\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur}\mid\last(\vec{w})\in V(p_{i})\}.

Then the
unravelling of
\mathcal{M}
from
s
is the
intuitionistic neighbourhood model

\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}:=(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur},N^{ur},V^{ur}).

We know that
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}
is coherent.
Before proving that an intuitionistic neighbourhood model
\mathcal{M}
and its
unravelling are related in a truth-preserving way, we verify that the
unravelling is Cartesian.

3.31 Lemma
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood
model and
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}=(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur},N^{ur},V^{ur})
its
unravelling from
s
.
Define
\vec{w}R\vec{v}
if
\vec{v}\in a(\vec{w})
for some
a\in N^{ur}
.

1.

If
\vec{w}\leq^{ur,\sim}\vec{v}

then
\length(\vec{w}_{nbd})=\length(\vec{v}_{nbd})
.

2.

If
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
and
\length(\vec{w})=\length(\vec{v})

then
\vec{w}=\vec{v}
.

3.

If
\vec{w}\leq^{ur,\sim}\vec{v}
and
\length(\vec{w})=\length(\vec{v})

then
\vec{w}=\vec{v}
.

4.

\vec{w}R^{\sim}\vec{v}
if and only if

\vec{w}_{ord}=\vec{v}_{ord}
.

Proof.

The first item follows from the fact that
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}

implies
\length(\vec{w}_{nbd})=\length(\vec{v}_{nbd})
, which holds
by definition of
\leq^{ur}
.
For the second item, suppose
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}

and
\length(\vec{w})=\length(\vec{v})
. Then

\length(\vec{w}_{nbd})=\length(\vec{v}_{nbd})
, hence

\length(\vec{w}_{ord})=\length(\vec{v}_{ord})
. The latter implies
that the order paths
\vec{t}_{0},\ldots,\vec{t}_{\length(\vec{w}_{nbd})}

have length 1, which forces
\vec{w}=\vec{v}
.
The third item follows from repeated application of the second.

Lastly, note that
\vec{w}R\vec{v}
if and only if

\vec{w}=(\vec{v}:a:x)
or
\vec{v}=(\vec{w}:a:x)
for some
suitable
a\in N
and
x\in W
. This entails that
\vec{w}R\vec{v}

implies
\vec{w}_{ord}=\vec{v}_{ord}
. Therefore

\vec{w}R^{\sim}\vec{v}
implies
\vec{w}_{ord}=\vec{v}_{ord}
.
Furthermore, we get that
\vec{w}_{ord}R^{\sim}\vec{w}
for any

\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur}
. Therefore
\vec{w}_{ord}=\vec{v}_{ord}

implies
\vec{w}R^{\sim}\vec{v}
.
∎

3.32 Proposition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be a coherent intuitionistic neighbourhood model
and
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}=(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur},N^{ur},V^{ur})
its unravelling
from
s
. Then
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}
is Cartesian.

Proof.

To see that
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}
is
R^{\sim}
-Cartesian,
suppose
\vec{w}\leq^{ur,\sim}\vec{v}R^{\sim}\vec{w}
.
Then
\vec{w}\leq^{ur,\sim}\vec{v}
implies
\length(\vec{w}_{nbd})=\length(\vec{v}_{nbd})
and
\vec{v}R^{\sim}\vec{w}
implies

\vec{v}_{ord}=\vec{w}_{ord}
.
Therefore
\length(\vec{w})=\length(\vec{v})
,
so that Lemma
3.31
(
3
) gives
\vec{w}=\vec{v}
.

Second, suppose
\vec{v}R^{\sim}\vec{u}
,
a_{\vec{w}}\in N^{ur}

and
\vec{v},\vec{u}\in\dom(a_{\vec{w}})
.
Then
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
and
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{u}
,
so
\vec{v}\leq^{ur,\sim}\vec{u}
.
This implies
\length(\vec{v}_{nbd})=\length(\vec{u}_{nbd})
.
Further,
\vec{v}R^{\sim}\vec{u}
implies that

\vec{v}_{ord}=\vec{u}_{ord}
.
It follows that
\length(\vec{v})=\length(\vec{u})
,
so we can use Lemma
3.31
(
3
)
to see that
\vec{v}=\vec{u}
.
Clearly this implies
a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{v})=a_{\vec{w}}(\vec{u})
,
so
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}
is N-Cartesian.
∎

Finally, we prove that the unravelling preserves truth,
in the following sense:

3.33 Proposition
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
be an intuitionistic neighbourhood
model,
s\in W
and
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur}=(W_{s}^{ur},\leq^{ur},N^{ur},V^{ur})

its unravelling from
s
.
Then for all
\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur}
and all
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
,

\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{w}\Vdash\varphi\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{M},\last(\vec{w})\Vdash\varphi.

Proof.

We use induction on the structure of
\varphi
.
If
\varphi=\bot
then this is clearly true, and the case
\varphi=p_{i}\in\Prop

follows from the definition of
V^{ur}
.
The inductive cases for
\wedge
and
\vee
are routine.

Induction step for
\varphi=\psi\to\chi
.

Suppose
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{w}\Vdash\psi\to\chi
.
Suppose
x
is such that
\last(\vec{v})\leq x
and
\mathcal{M},x\Vdash\psi
.
Then we can use (
N2
) repeatedly to find an unravelling
path
\vec{v}
such that
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
and
\last(\vec{v})=x
,
see Figure
4
.

s
\circ
\circ
\circ
\circ
x
\circ
a
a
\vec{w}
\rightsquigarrow
s
\circ
\circ
\circ
\circ
x
\circ
\circ
\circ
a
\vec{w}
\vec{v}

Figure 4:
From
\vec{w}\in W_{s}^{ur}
and
\last(\vec{w})\leq x
,
we can construct
\vec{v}\in W_{s}^{ur}
such that

\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
and
\last(\vec{v})=x
.

By the induction hypothesis we have
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{v}\Vdash\psi
,
and since
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
we must have
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{v}\Vdash\chi
.
Using the induction hypothesis again gives
\mathcal{M},x\Vdash\chi
.
Since
x
was any successor of
\last(\vec{w})
, it follows that

\mathcal{M},\last(\vec{w})\Vdash\psi\to\chi
.
Conversely, if
\mathcal{M},\last(\vec{w})\Vdash\psi\to\chi

and
\vec{v}\in W_{s}^{ur}
is such that
\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}

and
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{v}\Vdash\psi
, then we have

\last(\vec{w})\leq\last(\vec{v})
and we can use induction to find

\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{v}\Vdash\chi
. Therefore
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{w}\Vdash\psi\to\chi
.

We conclude that
\mathcal{M},\last(\vec{w})\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
.

Now suppose
\mathcal{M},\last(\vec{w})\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
.
Let
\vec{v}\in W_{s}^{ur}
and
b\in N^{ur}
be such that

\vec{w}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
and
\vec{v}\in\dom(b)
.
Then
b
is of the form
a_{\vec{u}}
for some
\vec{u}\leq^{ur}\vec{v}
.
By definition we have
\last(\vec{w})\leq\last(\vec{v})
and

\last(\vec{v})\in\dom(a)
.
The induction hypothesis then gives us some
y\in W
such that

y\in a(\last(\vec{v}))
and
\mathcal{M},y\Vdash\psi
.
But this means that
(\vec{v}:a:y)\in a_{\vec{u}}(\vec{v})

and
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},(\vec{v}:a:y)\Vdash\psi
.
Therefore
\mathcal{M}_{s}^{ur},\vec{w}\Vdash\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\psi
.
∎

3.34 Theorem
.

We have
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\models\varphi
.

Proof.

If
\Gamma\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
then it follows from
Proposition
3.8
that
\Gamma\models\varphi
.
If
\Gamma\not\Vdash^{\mathrm{inm}}\varphi
then there exists an intuitionistic neighbourhood
model
\mathcal{M}=(W,\leq,N,V)
and a world
w\in W
such that

\mathcal{M},w\Vdash\Gamma
and
\mathcal{M},w\not\Vdash\varphi
.
By Theorem
3.14
we may assume that
\mathcal{M}
is coherent.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{w}^{ur}
be the unravelling of
\mathcal{M}
from
w
,
and write
\vec{w}
for the unravelling path consisting just of
w
.
Then Proposition
3.33
tells us
\mathcal{M}_{w}^{ur},\vec{w}\Vdash\Gamma

while
\mathcal{M}_{w}^{ur},\vec{w}\not\Vdash\varphi
.
Now it follows from Corollary
3.23
that

(\mathcal{M}_{w}^{ur})^{\circ}
is a
\mathsf{IFOM}
-structure which shows that

\Gamma\not\models\varphi
.
∎

3.5  Canonical model construction

We construct a canonical model to derive strong completeness of
\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}

with respect to the class intuitionistic monotone models.
This then proves that the calculus defining
\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}
axiomatises
\mathsf{IM}
.
As usual in the intuitionistic setting, the canonical model is based on
the set of prime theories ordered by inclusion.

3.35 Definition
.

A
prime theory
is
a set
\Gamma\subseteq\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
such that,

•

\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}}\varphi
implies
\varphi\in\Delta

(
\Gamma
is deductively closed);

•

\varphi\vee\psi\in\Gamma
implies
\varphi\in\Gamma
or
\psi\in\Gamma

(
\Gamma
has disjunctive property);

•

\bot\notin\Gamma
(
\Gamma
is consistent).

A prime theory
\Gamma
is called
maximal
if it is not properly contained
in any other prime theory.

We can prove a Lindenbaum lemma as usual, see for example
[
3
, Lemma 11]
.

3.36 Lemma
.

Let
\Gamma\cup\{\varphi\}\subseteq\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
be a set of formulas such that

\Gamma\not\vdash\varphi
.
Then we can extend
\Gamma
to a prime theory
\Gamma^{\prime}
such that

\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\varphi\notin\Gamma^{\prime}
.

The intuitionistic Kripke frame underlying our canonical model is standard
except for a small change: we unravel each maximal prime theory into
a chain of copies. This is necessary to make the diamond-case of the
truth lemma go through. To this end, the worlds of the canonical model
are given by pairs
(\Gamma,n)
, where
\Gamma
is a prime theory and

n
is a natural number. If
\Gamma
is not maximal then we only allow
n=0
,
but if
\Gamma
is maximal then
n
ranges over
\mathbb{N}
.
The intuitionistic neighbourhoods on the resulting intuitionistic Kripke
frame are designed such that the model satisfies and falsifies formulas
of the form
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.6pt{\vbox to5.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.7986pt\lower-2.7986pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi
and
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

at the right worlds.
For formulas of the form
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.52pt{\vbox to6.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.25974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\varphi
we use many different neighbourhoods.
While we could do with fewer intuitionistic neighbourhoods, the current
setup makes the proof of the truth lemma go through more smoothly.

3.37 Definition
.

The domain of our canonical model is given by

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}}:=\{(\Gamma,0)\mid\Gamma\text{ is a non-maximal prime theory}\}\cup\{(\Gamma,n)\mid\Gamma\text{ is a maximal prime theory and }n\in\mathbb{N}\}.

Order
W_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}}
pointwise, that is, let
(\Gamma,n)\mathbin{\subset\kern-7.4pt<}(\Gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime})

if
\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
n\leq n^{\prime}
.
This gives an intuitionistic Kripke frame
(W_{\mathsf{IM_{Calc}}},\mathbin{\subset\kern-7.4pt<})
.
For each
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.92pt{\vbox to3.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.95903pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.56pt{\vbox to4.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.28183pt\lower-2.28183pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
